9
Interpretations of the differential distributions and production cross-sections

9.1
Constraints on Higgs boson couplings within the
\kappa
-framework

To probe physics beyond the SM, the measured production-mode cross-sections are interpreted in the
\kappa
framework
[
135
]
, which parameterises possible deviations of Higgs boson couplings from their SM values using coupling-strength modifiers
\vec{\kappa}
. Under the narrow-width approximation, Higgs production and decay factorise, and
\sigma\cdot\mathcal{B}
can be expressed in terms of modified Higgs production cross-sections and partial widths. The
\vec{\kappa}
dependence at
\sqrt{s}=13.6\,\text{TeV}
is obtained by linear interpolation between the corresponding terms at 13 and 14
TeV

[
41
]
.

The interpretation is performed in a benchmark scenario with two universal modifiers:
\kappa_{V}=\kappa_{W}=\kappa_{Z}
for Higgs-to-vector-boson couplings and
\kappa_{f}=\kappa_{t}=\kappa_{b}=\kappa_{c}=\kappa_{\tau}=\kappa_{\mu}
for Higgs-to-fermion couplings, assuming a single Higgs boson state that is even under the combined charge-parity (CP) transformation, with the SM tensor structure and no undetected or invisible decays. For loop-induced processes, the resolved treatment is adopted, in which the effective loop couplings are expressed in terms of the modified couplings to the massive SM particles.

The results are statistically combined with the corresponding measurements performed with the full Run 2 data set
[
8
]
, accounting for the systematic uncertainties described in Section
7
. The following experimental uncertainties are treated as correlated between Run 2 and Run 3 results: the muon momentum scale and reconstruction efficiency; the electron energy scale and energy resolution; and the jet energy scale and resolution, except for Run 3-specific components. Other experimental uncertainties are treated as uncorrelated. Theoretical uncertainties from missing higher-order corrections affecting the Higgs boson signal cross-sections in exclusive kinematic regions, the Higgs boson mass uncertainty, and uncertainties in background estimates are treated as correlated. All other theoretical uncertainties, including PDF and parton shower uncertainties, are treated as uncorrelated.

The likelihood contours and best-fit values in the
(\kappa_{f},\kappa_{V})
plane are shown in Figure
15
. Only the quadrant with
\kappa_{f}>0
and
\kappa_{V}>0
is displayed, as the
H\to ZZ^{*}\to 4\ell
channel has limited sensitivity to the relative sign of the two modifiers. The combined measurements improves on the previous result
[
8
]
by about 40% (25%) on
\kappa_{V}
(
\kappa_{f}
). The compatibility of the combined measurement with the SM expectation corresponds to a
p
-value of 29%.

Figure 15:
Likelihood contours in the
(\kappa_{f},\kappa_{V})
plane comparing the current results with those obtained in Run 2. The statistical combination of the two measurements is also presented. The area outside the solid (dashed) contours is excluded at 95% (68%) confidence level. The best-fit point (solid cross) is shown together with the SM prediction (star).

9.2
Constraints on CP-even and CP-odd effective field theory operators

The
\kappa
-framework assumes that the tensor structure of the Higgs boson couplings is the same as in the SM. To probe possible non-SM tensor structures, selected unfolded differential measurements are interpreted in an effective field theory (EFT) approach. In this approach, the BSM interactions are introduced via additional higher-dimensional operators

\mathcal{O}_{i}^{(d)}
of dimension
d
, supplementing the SM Lagrangian
\cal{L}_{\textrm{SM}}
,

\mathcal{L}_{\textrm{EFT}}=\mathcal{L}_{\textrm{SM}}+\sum_{i}\frac{C_{i}^{(d)}}{\Lambda^{(d-4)}}\mathcal{O}_{i}^{(d)}\ \ \ \text{for}\ d>4,

where
C_{i}^{(d)}
are the Wilson coefficients and
\Lambda
denotes the characteristic scale of new physics. In this analysis, only dimension-six operators are considered. Dimension-five and dimension-seven operators are not included, as they violate lepton or baryon number conservation and are therefore not relevant in the present context. Although dimension-eight operators can interfere with the SM amplitudes at a same order as the quadratic contributions from dimension-six operators, their effect is not considered. The data are sensitive only to the combinations
c_{i}=C_{i}^{(d=6)}/\Lambda^{2}
, which are the quantities constrained in this analysis.

Constraints are derived within the Standard Model EFT (SMEFT) formalism
[
136
]
in the Warsaw basis
[
137
]
. Given the sensitivity in the
H\to ZZ^{*}\to 4\ell
channel, only three CP-even and three CP-odd operators modifying Higgs boson interactions with electroweak gauge bosons are probed as summarised in Table
6
. Operators affecting only double-Higgs boson production, as well as operators modifying Higgs couplings to quarks and leptons, are not considered. The impact of these operators on the total Higgs boson width is neglected.

Table 6:
Summary of CP-even and CP-odd EFT operators in the SMEFT formalism probed in the
H\to ZZ^{*}\to 4\ell
channel. The corresponding tensor structure in terms of the SM fields from Ref.
[
136
]
is shown together with the associated Wilson coefficients and the affected interaction vertices.
V_{\mu\nu}
is the field-strength tensor for a given gauge field
V=W,B
.

Operator

Structure

Coeff.

\mathcal{O}_{HW}

HH^{\dagger}W^{l}_{\mu\nu}W^{\mu\nu l}

c_{HW}

\mathcal{O}_{HB}

HH^{\dagger}B_{\mu\nu}B^{\mu\nu}

c_{HB}

\mathcal{O}_{HWB}

HH^{\dagger}\tau^{l}W^{l}_{\mu\nu}B^{\mu\nu}

c_{HWB}

\mathcal{O}_{H\tilde{W}}

HH^{\dagger}\tilde{W}_{\mu\nu}^{l}W^{\mu\nu l}

c_{H\tilde{W}}

\mathcal{O}_{H\tilde{B}}

HH^{\dagger}\tilde{B}_{\mu\nu}B^{\mu\nu}

c_{H\tilde{B}}

\mathcal{O}_{H\tilde{W}B}

HH^{\dagger}\tau^{l}\tilde{W}_{\mu\nu}^{l}B^{\mu\nu}

c_{H\tilde{W}B}

The EFT interpretation is performed using observables selected to enhance sensitivity to the targeted operators and to retain discrimination between the interference (linear) and BSM-squared (quadratic) contributions. The two-dimensional
(p_{\textrm{T}}^{4\ell},m_{34})
measurement is used to constrain
c_{HW}
, since it simultaneously probes production- and decay-side effects:
p_{\textrm{T}}^{4\ell}
is sensitive to modifications of the production kinematics, while
m_{34}
provides sensitivity to changes in the decay matrix element in
H\to ZZ^{*}\to 4\ell
. The sensitivity of this approach depends on the chosen binning in
m_{34}
as the balance between statistical precision and shape sensitivity is required to be optimised.

For the remaining CP-even operators, the angular observable
\phi
provides improved sensitivity compared with
(p_{\textrm{T}}^{4\ell},m_{34})
. This improvement arises as the linear SMEFT contribution exhibits sign changes as a function of
\phi
, leading to a periodic modulation that cancels when integrated over the full phase space. Consequently, the SM-BSM interference is suppressed in
\phi
-inclusive observables, similarly to those observed in non-Higgs boson decays
[
138
,
139
]
. Although
\phi
is a CP-even observable, it also exhibits sensitivity to CP-odd operators, with the interference term showing an analogous behaviour to the CP-even case but with different periodicity. The
\Delta\phi_{jj}
observable is also studied for CP-odd operators, but it does not provide discrimination between CP-even and CP-odd operators in the present dataset due to the dominant dependence on quadratic EFT terms.

The Wilson coefficients are constrained by comparing the unfolded signal cross-section in each bin of the chosen distribution to the SMEFT expectation, with the expected cross-section and acceptance parametrised using a matrix-element-based procedure
[
140
]
. For a bin
j
of a given observable, the expected cross-section is written as

\sigma_{pp\to H\to 4\ell,j}^{\mathrm{SMEFT}}\!\left(c_{i}\right)=\sigma_{pp\to H\to 4\ell,j}^{\mathrm{SM}}\cdot\sum_{p}{\left(1+\sum_{i}{\alpha_{i}^{(p,j)}c_{i}}+\sum_{i,k\geq i}{\beta_{ik}^{(p,j)}c_{i}c_{k}}\right)}\cdot\frac{\left(1+\sum_{i}{\alpha_{i}^{(\Gamma_{4\ell},j)}c_{i}}+\sum_{i,k\geq i}{\beta_{ik}^{(\Gamma_{4\ell},j)}c_{i}c_{k}}\right)}{\left(1+\sum_{i}{\alpha_{i}^{(\Gamma_{tot},j)}c_{i}}+\sum_{i,k\geq i}{\beta_{ik}^{(\Gamma_{tot},j)}c_{i}c_{k}}\right)}\,,

(5)

where
p
labels Higgs boson production processes, and
\Gamma_{4\ell}
and
\Gamma_{tot}
denote the partial width to four leptons and the total width, respectively. The terms linear in
c_{i}
encode SM–EFT interference, while the quadratic terms represent the pure dimension-six contribution. The coefficients
\alpha
and
\beta
are obtained from matrix elements computed using dedicated simulated samples generated at LO in QCD with
MadGraph5_aMC@NLO
and the NNPDF23lo PDF set. The SMEFT contributions to
\mathrm{VBF}
production and the
HZZ
decay vertex are modelled using
SMEFTsim

[
136
,
141
]
. The SMEFT impact on
VH
production is neglected, as it has a negligible effect on the considered distributions.

Since the measurements do not provide sufficient sensitivity to constrain the full set of coefficients simultaneously, fits are performed for one coefficient at a time, with all others set to zero. Two treatments of the SMEFT expansion are presented: a
linear-only
interpretation retaining only the interference term, and a
linear+quadratic
interpretation including both interference and BSM-squared terms. Quadratic terms typically increase sensitivity through additional changes in total cross-section, and a comparison of the two treatments provides information on the impact of higher-order contributions and the possible onset of perturbative breakdown as quadratic effects become dominant.
For each probed coefficient, one-dimensional likelihood scans are performed using the observable providing the best expected sensitivity:
(p_{\textrm{T}}^{4\ell},m_{34})
for
c_{HW}
and
\phi
for rest. The observed and expected confidence intervals (CL) are summarised in Table
7
, and the corresponding likelihood scans comparing the linear-only and linear+quadratic treatments are shown for the CP-even Wilson Coefficients in Figure
16
. Additional results where combinations of two EFT operators are simultaneously probed are in shown in Figure
17
. For each combination, the scans are obtained from the observable that provides the best expected sensitivity. Two-dimensional EFT scans are not used to derive limits, as the likelihood exhibits poorly constrained directions in parameter space, leading to degeneracies and flat directions.

The corresponding limits on the new physics scale
\Lambda
for benchmark coefficient values are shown in Figure
18
.

$164\text{\,}\mathrm{f}\mathrm{b}$^{-1}
at
\sqrt{s}=13.6\penalty\ \text{TeV}
, assuming
\Lambda=1\penalty\ \text{TeV}
. Only one Wilson coefficient is fitted at a time while all others are set to zero. For each coefficient, results obtained in the linear-only and linear+quadratic SMEFT treatments are presented.
(p_{\textrm{T}}^{4\ell},m_{34})
observable is used for
c_{HW}
results, while
\phi
is used for the other operators.

9.3
Constraints on Higgs boson self-coupling

The trilinear Higgs self-coupling,
\lambda_{3}
, enters single-Higgs production and decay at the one-loop level through electroweak corrections, and can therefore be constrained using precision measurements of single-Higgs observables. These effects modify both the overall rate and, in particular, the shape of kinematic distributions that probe hard radiation, making the Higgs boson transverse-momentum spectrum especially sensitive to
\lambda_{3}

[
142
]
. For this reason, both the differential measurement of
p_{\textrm{T}}^{4\ell}
in the fiducial phase space and the STXS measurements, which rely on different event categorisations and modelling assumptions, can be used to probe the effects of the self-coupling, allowing a comparison of the sensitivity achieved by the two complementary approaches.

The interpretation is performed in the
\kappa
-framework by introducing the modifier
\kappa_{\lambda}\equiv\lambda_{3}/\lambda_{3}^{\mathrm{SM}}
, with
\kappa_{\lambda}=1
corresponding to the SM expectation and setting all other couplings to their SM expectations.

The dependence of an observable
\Sigma
on the trilinear Higgs self-coupling is parametrised following Ref.
[
142
]
as

\Sigma^{\mathrm{BSM}}_{\lambda_{3}}(\kappa_{\lambda})=Z_{H}^{\mathrm{BSM}}\,\Sigma_{\mathrm{LO}}\left(1+\kappa_{\lambda}\,C_{1}(\{p_{n}\})+\delta Z_{H}\right),

(6)

where
\Sigma_{\mathrm{LO}}
denotes the leading-order prediction, including factorisable higher-order QCD corrections. The factor
Z_{H}^{\mathrm{BSM}}
represents the universal Higgs wave-function renormalisation, which accounts for corrections induced by the modified self-coupling. The coefficient
C_{1}(\{p_{n}\})
encodes the finite one-loop contribution arising from the modified trilinear interaction and depends on the kinematics of the process, denoted collectively by
\{p_{n}\}
.
The factor
Z_{H}^{\mathrm{BSM}}
is expressed in terms of
\delta Z_{H}
and represents the NLO term
[
142
]
, as

Z_{H}^{\mathrm{BSM}}=\frac{1}{1-(\kappa_{\lambda}^{2}-1)\,\delta Z_{H}}\,.

(7)

In this interpretation, all other coupling modifiers are fixed to their SM values, and the
\kappa_{\lambda}
dependence is implemented as a reweighting of the expected signal yields in each analysis bin, using
C_{1}
values evaluated in the relevant fiducial or STXS phase space. The
C_{1}
coefficients are obtained with the automated reweighting procedure of Ref.
[
142
]
for all production-modes supported by the tool, and supplemented for
\mathrm{ggF}
using the dedicated calculation at
\sqrt{s}=13.6\penalty\ \text{TeV}

[
143
]
. The Higgs boson decay contribution is taken to be weakly dependent on kinematics
[
142
]
, and the corresponding total-width contribution is taken from Ref.
[
144
]
. The range of the validity for the given
C_{1}
and
\delta Z_{H}
is
|\kappa_{\lambda}|<
20.

Complementary constraints on
\kappa_{\lambda}
are derived from the differential
p_{\textrm{T}}^{4\ell}
and the STXS production-mode measurements.
The corresponding likelihood scans are compared in Figure
19
, yielding observed 95% CL intervals of
[-8.8,\,19.9]
and
[-9.0,\,20.9]
, respectively, compatible with expected 95% CL intervals of
[-8.8,\,19.9]
. The double-minimum structure of the likelihood arises from the quadratic dependence of the cross-section on
\kappa_{\lambda}
. The observed limit from the STXS measurement is slightly weaker than that obtained from the differential
p_{\textrm{T}}^{4\ell}
measurement. This difference is driven by the strong sensitivity of the
\kappa_{\lambda}
interpretation to the
\mathrm{ggF}
production mode and by the fact that the observed
\mathrm{ggF}
signal strength in the STXS measurement is lower than the inclusive signal strength.

Figure 19:
Observed negative-log-likelihood scan as a function of the trilinear Higgs self-coupling modifier
\kappa_{\lambda}
, comparing the constraints obtained from the STXS production-mode measurement (orange line) and from the fiducial differential measurement based on
p_{\textrm{T}}^{4\ell}
(light blue line). The expected results are plotted together for measurements due to their similar sensitivity (black dashed line). The curves are shown relative to their respective minima. The Standard Model prediction corresponds to
\kappa_{\lambda}=1
.

10
Conclusion

A measurement of inclusive and differential Higgs boson production cross-sections in the
H\rightarrow ZZ^{*}\rightarrow 4\ell
channel is presented using
$164\text{\,}\mathrm{f}\mathrm{b}$^{-1}
of
\sqrt{s}=13.6\penalty\ \text{TeV}

pp
collision data recorded by the ATLAS detector at the LHC between 2022 and 2024.
These results constitute the most comprehensive ATLAS measurements of Higgs boson production in the
H\rightarrow ZZ^{*}\rightarrow 4\ell
channel at
\sqrt{s}=13.6\penalty\ \text{TeV}
to date, with a precision already comparable to that obtained using the full Run 2 dataset at
\sqrt{s}=13\penalty\ \text{TeV}
. The analysis exploits the data using improved analysis and ML techniques in two complementary frameworks, measuring fiducial cross-sections with minimal theoretical assumptions and production-mode and STXS cross-sections to separate the contributions from different Higgs boson production mechanisms.
The inclusive fiducial cross-section is measured to be
\sigma_{\textrm{fid}}=3.65^{+0.35}_{-0.33}\penalty\ \textrm{fb}
, in agreement with the Standard Model prediction of
\sigma_{\textrm{fid}}^{\textrm{SM}}=3.68\pm 0.17\penalty\ \textrm{fb}
, and is extrapolated to a total cross-section of
\sigma_{\textrm{total}}=59.3\pm 5.7\penalty\ \textrm{pb}
, also consistent with the Standard Model and previous results. Differential cross-sections are measured as a function of several Higgs boson production and decay observables and are found to be in agreement with state-of-the-art SM predictions.
Higgs boson candidate events are categorised into regions sensitive to different production mechanisms and are used to measure production-mode cross-sections and a Reduced STXS Stage 1.2 scheme. A product of inclusive production cross-section and branching ratio into
ZZ^{\ast}
of
\sigma_{|y_{H}|<2.5}\cdot\mathcal{B}\left(H\rightarrow ZZ^{\ast}\right)=1.42\pm 0.16\penalty\ \textrm{pb}
is obtained for Higgs boson rapidities below
2.5
, consistent with the Standard Model prediction of
\sigma_{|y_{H}|<2.5}^{\textrm{SM}}\cdot\mathcal{B}^{\textrm{SM}}\left(H\rightarrow ZZ^{\ast}\right)=1.44\pm 0.08\penalty\ \textrm{pb}
.
The precision of both the differential and STXS measurements is comparable to that achieved by ATLAS at
\sqrt{s}=13\penalty\ \text{TeV}
with Run 2 data
[
8
,
9
]
, and improves by approximately a factor of two relative to the first ATLAS results at
\sqrt{s}=13.6\penalty\ \text{TeV}
in this channel
[
20
]
.
The differential measurements as a function of
\phi
and
p_{\mathrm{T}}^{4\ell}\penalty\ \mathrm{vs.}\penalty\ m_{34}
are used to constrain effective field theory operators and are found to be compatible with SM expectations.
The production-mode results are further interpreted in the
\kappa
framework and combined with Run 2 measurements
[
8
]
, yielding best-fit values consistent with Run 2 and about 40% (25%) improvement on
\kappa_{V}
(
\kappa_{f}
) limits.
Limits are also derived on the trilinear Higgs self-coupling, and are consistent with the Standard Model.
